26 auto-cleared for voucher issuance ($138.82 in Ryde vouchers) and 17 with no refund due. Each one passed the pre-execution policy check.

Where it plugs in

Inputs

What the court reads

trip · GPS · chat · fare · profiles · OneMap route · LTA traffic · weather

Outputs

What it hands back

cleared / needs a person · proposed actions · plain-language notices · audit receipt

Production connectors (not included)

Where Ryde would connect

  • trip records in → one case record
  • refund → wallet / voucher API
  • account flag → trust and safety queue
  • appeals → audit receipt

Route deviation · B-0002

Detour · consent given beforehand

30.2 s
Dispute statement

The driver took a longer way round than the app showed and I paid more than the estimate. I want the difference back.

Trip summary

RydeTAXI Clarke Quay → Bedok Mall, estimate 15.5 km / 21.8 min, pickup 12:14 drop-off 12:46; charged $41.09 (metered)

Proposed outcome

Human review

Send to a human reviewer: decide the RD-5 consent-scope question for the 12:29–12:39 segment (did the rider's agreement to "go round via Sims Avenue" cover the driven path, when no fact ties that path to Sims Avenue?) and, if not exempt, refund $4.61 (3.27 km × $1.4098/km) as a Ryde voucher under NS-7; do not disturb the $0.11 metered waiting unit or the $1.47 platform fee; no account flag.

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Actual distance 19.4 km vs optimal route 15.5 km: +3.9 km (+25%).
  • F2 Trip duration 32.2 min vs estimated 21.8 min (+10.4 min).
  • F3 Off-route segment 12:29–12:39: 6.34 km driven where the optimal route covers 3.07 km, adding 3.27 km (up to 755 m from the optimal route).
  • F4 Excess distance billed $5.54 (3.93 km × $1.4098/km, this trip's distance fare ÷ distance km).
  • F5 Waiting fee billed $0.11 (0.4 min × $0.26/min).
  • F6 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F7 Total charged $41.09 SGD: base $3.90, distance $27.35 (19.40 km), time $8.26 (31.8 min), waiting $0.11, platform $1.47, discount $0.00.
  • F8 Chat #1 12:26 driver: “Traffic ahead on Pan Island Expressway is building up, ok if I go round via Sims Avenue?”
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • This is a RydeTAXI trip (metered), so deviation distance flows straight into the fare charged; the ride covered 19.4 km against an optimal 15.5 km, i.e. +3.9 km (+25%), and 32.2 min against an estimated 21.8 min.
  • The GPS track records one off-route segment, 12:29–12:39, driving 6.34 km where the optimal route covers 3.07 km and adding 3.27 km, straying up to 755 m from the optimal route; nothing in the record exempts it.
Driver's advocate
  • The single off-route segment the rider is charged for is the 12:29–12:39 loop of 6.34 km against 3.07 km optimal, adding 3.27 km (F3); the driver asked the rider before leaving the optimal route whether he could go round via Sims Avenue (F8, 12:26) and the rider answered "Sure, go ahead." at 12:27 (F9), i.e. the rider's own message precedes the segment start of 12:29 (F3), so RD-5 consent exists.
  • The consent in F9 is a specific route authorisation that named a road - Sims Avenue (F8) - and it is tied to the driven segment by time: the message of 12:27 sits immediately before the segment that runs 12:29–12:39 (F3, F9), so on the record the loop is the Sims Avenue loop the rider approved; the driver's own post-trip statement (F11) is consistent but is not relied on as consent under G11.
Policy trace
  • ✓ RD-1 off-route segment(s) exist [case] 1 segment(s) (F3)
  • ✗ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F3] no qualifying incident (speed band alone does not exempt) (no evidence)
  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F3] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 12:29:55 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount. Evidence - F8: Chat #1 12:26 driver: “Traffic ahead on Pan Island Expressway is building up, ok if I go round via Sims Avenue?”; F9: Chat #2 12:27 rider: “Sure, go ahead.”; F11: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “Traffic was building up ahead so I asked the rider before turning off via Sims Avenue and the rider agreed.” (F8, F9, F11)
  • ? RD-3 rider-requested stop; whether the rider named this place [F3] RULE (RD-3(a)): exempt if a RIDER message before the segment named a specific place and the car stopped there (the whole way there is exempt; how long the stop lasted or how far the place was does not reduce it). A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (F8, F9)
  • ✗ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 0 (F17)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. no hint word; read the messages anyway, a threat needs no keyword (no evidence)
  • allowed outcomes: refund_partial, no_action, escalate; amount = Σ extra_km of non-exempt segments × $1.4098/km (distance_fare ÷ distance_km), rounded to cents
  • flag_account required: False
Why automation stopped

segment finding undetermined for F3

What would change this ruling
  • F3: the record does not settle whether the rider's agreement covers this segment; a person decides whether $4.61 is refunded.
  • Had the driver carried a prior upheld route dispute in the last 90 days, RD-6 would add an account flag.
Notices
Notice to the rider
Your dispute on trip B-0002 has been passed to a Ryde reviewer. No decision has been made yet: nothing has been refunded or charged as part of this review. The reviewer will look at what was agreed about the route and which stretch it covered. Ryde will contact you once the review is complete.
Notice to the driver
The dispute on trip B-0002 has been passed to a Ryde reviewer. No decision has been made yet, and nothing changes on your account or earnings as part of this review. The reviewer will look at what was agreed about the route and which stretch it covered. Ryde will contact you once the review is complete.

Route deviation · B-0013

Two detours · first one agreed

31.3 s
Dispute statement

I agreed to go round the jam near the start, but later he went off the route a second time without asking. I want that part refunded.

Trip summary

RydeTAXI Woodlands Civic Centre → Orchard Road, estimate 20.6 km / 28.8 min, pickup 18:31 drop-off 19:18; charged $61.32 (metered)

Proposed outcome

Human review

Refer to a human reviewer: decide whether the 18:46–18:56 segment (F3) is covered by the 18:43 rider message, then refund $6.74 for the 19:03–19:12 segment if F3 is found exempt, or $14.72 if it is not; no account flag.

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Actual distance 31.0 km vs optimal route 20.6 km: +10.4 km (+50%).
  • F2 Trip duration 47.3 min vs estimated 28.8 min (+18.5 min).
  • F3 Off-route segment 18:46–18:56: 7.10 km driven where the optimal route covers 1.43 km, adding 5.66 km (up to 1923 m from the optimal route).
  • F4 Off-route segment 19:03–19:12: 5.77 km driven where the optimal route covers 1.00 km, adding 4.78 km (up to 1680 m from the optimal route).
  • F5 Excess distance billed $14.64 (10.38 km × $1.4100/km, this trip's distance fare ÷ distance km).
  • F6 Waiting fee billed $0.11 (0.4 min × $0.26/min).
  • F7 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F8 Total charged $61.32 SGD: base $3.90, distance $43.64 (30.95 km), time $12.20 (46.9 min), waiting $0.11, platform $1.47, discount $0.00.
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • This is a metered RydeTAXI trip, so extra kilometres drove the fare: the record shows excess distance billed $14.64 computed at $1.4100/km (F5), and the total charged $61.32 already includes $43.64 in distance fare (F8).
  • The trip produced two off-route segments: 18:46–18:56 adding 5.66 km (F3) and 19:03–19:12 adding 4.78 km (F4); together 10.44 km, matching the +10.4 km (+50%) excess over the 20.6 km optimal route (F1) and the +18.5 min duration excess (F2).
Driver's advocate
  • The first off-route segment (18:46–18:56, F3) is covered by the rider's own prior message: at 18:42 the driver asked 'ok if I go round via Rifle Range Road?' and at 18:43 the rider replied 'Sure, your call.' (F9, F10), i.e. a rider-sent message before the segment started, so under RD-5 that segment is not a deviation.
  • The 18:43 consent is a named-road consent limited to Rifle Range Road; the record contains no road-name fact, stop or positioning message tying the 18:46–18:56 driven segment to Rifle Range Road, and nothing places it elsewhere, so the ground for F3 is named_road_untied - the judge may find the segment undetermined (G13), not a driver errand.

The second turn-off was my GPS rerouting.” (F9, F10, F12)

segment finding undetermined for F3

Route deviation · B-0017

Detour on a fixed fare · consent given

26.6 s
Dispute statement

The driver took a longer way round than the app showed. The fare was a fixed quote, but I want the detour looked at.

Trip summary

RydeX Punggol Waterway Point → Tampines Mall, estimate 9.8 km / 15.1 min, pickup 09:59 drop-off 10:20; charged $23.18 (fixed)

Proposed outcome

Human review

Refer the F3 off-route segment to a human reviewer to determine whether the driven 10:04–10:11 loop corresponds to Pasir Ris Industrial Drive 1 as agreed in F8; no refund is executable on this fixed-quote RydeX fare.

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Actual distance 12.4 km vs optimal route 9.8 km: +2.6 km (+26%).
  • F2 Trip duration 21.8 min vs estimated 15.1 min (+6.7 min).
  • F3 Off-route segment 10:04–10:11: 3.98 km driven where the optimal route covers 1.77 km, adding 2.21 km (up to 1039 m from the optimal route).
  • F4 Billed distance 9.8 km does not exceed the optimal route (9.8 km).
  • F5 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F6 Total charged $23.18 SGD: base $3.90, distance $13.88 (9.84 km), time $3.93 (15.1 min), waiting $0.00, platform $1.47, discount $0.00.
  • F7 Chat #1 10:01 driver: “Jam on Tampines Expressway is building up ahead, can I go round via Pasir Ris Industrial Drive 1?”
  • F8 Chat #2 10:01 rider: “Sure, no problem.”
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The trip was longer than the optimal route by 2.6 km (+26%) on a 9.8 km estimate, and took 21.8 min against a 15.1 min estimate.
  • There is exactly one off-route segment, 10:04–10:11, where 3.98 km was driven against 1.77 km of optimal route, adding 2.21 km and straying up to 1039 m from the optimal route; this is an unexempted detour segment under RD-1 unless RD-4 or RD-5 excuses it.
Driver's advocate
  • RydeX is a fixed-quote, non-metered service: the rider was charged a flat $23.18 built from base, distance, time and platform fee, and the billed distance of 9.8 km equals the optimal route distance, so the 2.6 km actually driven added nothing to the fare.
  • Because the fare is a fixed quote, the only items that could be refunded under RD-2 are ERP/toll charges or unpublished post-trip add-ons; the fare breakdown shows a waiting fee of $0.00, no toll line and no other add-on, so the refundable amount is $0.00 and the correct outcome for the deviation itself is no_action.

  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F3] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 10:04:50 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes no amount (fixed fare) and no flag: if you cannot settle it, undetermined is allowed and the case may still be ruled. A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (F7, F8)

32.9 s
Dispute statement

Trip summary

RydeTAXI Buona Vista MRT → Pasir Ris MRT, estimate 27.4 km / 38.0 min, pickup 18:11 drop-off 18:58; charged $59.88 (metered)

Proposed outcome

Human review

Do not withhold before a person has read it.

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Actual distance 29.8 km vs optimal route 27.4 km: +2.4 km (+9%).
  • F2 Trip duration 47.9 min vs estimated 38.0 min (+9.9 min).
  • F3 Off-route segment 18:37–18:46: 5.69 km driven where the optimal route covers 3.62 km, adding 2.07 km (up to 1240 m from the optimal route).
  • F4 Excess distance billed $3.47 (2.46 km × $1.4101/km, this trip's distance fare ÷ distance km).
  • F5 Waiting fee billed $0.11 (0.4 min × $0.26/min).
  • F6 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F7 Total charged $59.88 SGD: base $3.90, distance $42.05 (29.82 km), time $12.35 (47.5 min), waiting $0.11, platform $1.47, discount $0.00.
  • F8 Chat #1 18:35 driver: “Jam on Pan Island Expressway is building up ahead, want me to take another way?”
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The GPS record shows one off-route segment on this RydeTAXI trip, 18:37–18:46, where 5.69 km were driven over a stretch the optimal route covers in 3.62 km, adding 2.07 km and straying up to 1240 m from the optimal route; under RD-1 this unexplained deviation breaches the driver's shortest-and-most-direct-route duty.
  • The rider's only message before the segment was '随便你，哪条快走哪条，我赶时间' at 18:35 (F9), i.e. a general route choice ('whichever way is faster') and not consent to any named road, so at most it is a general route authorisation under RD-5 - it does not cover a driver's own detour or a loop that reaches nowhere.
Driver's advocate
  • The single off-route segment (F3, 18:37–18:46, +2.07 km) was taken under the rider's own general route authorisation: the driver asked at 18:35 whether to take another way because of PIE congestion (F8) and the rider replied '随便你，哪条快走哪条，我赶时间' (F9), i.e. 'whichever way is faster', sent 2 minutes BEFORE the segment started at 18:37, so the pre-departure agreement required by RD-5 is satisfied and this segment is not a detour.
  • The driver's reason for the reroute is corroborated by the system record, not by his own words: the PAN ISLAND EXPRESSWAY was recorded in speed band 2 (10–19 km/h) during 18:00–18:37 (F12) on the optimal route, while the alternative expressway used was running freely at band 5 / 40–49 km/h (F13) - this is RD-4(b) supporting evidence of 'severe congestion' on the substituted optimal route, and there is no incident (F15) or weather (F16) factor to weigh against it.
Policy trace
  • ✓ RD-1 off-route segment(s) exist [case] 1 segment(s) (F3)
  • ✗ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F3] no qualifying incident (speed band alone does not exempt) (no evidence)
  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F3] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 18:37:05 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount. Evidence - F8: Chat #1 18:35 driver: “Jam on Pan Island Expressway is building up ahead, want me to take another way?”; F9: Chat #2 18:35 rider: “随便你，哪条快走哪条，我赶时间。”; F11: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “Traffic was building up ahead. I asked the rider and the rider said any faster way was fine, so I went round.” (F8, F9, F11)
  • ? RD-3 rider-requested stop; whether the rider named this place [F3] RULE (RD-3(a)): exempt if a RIDER message before the segment named a specific place and the car stopped there (the whole way there is exempt; how long the stop lasted or how far the place was does not reduce it). A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (F8, F9)
  • ✓ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 2 (F18)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. no hint word; read the messages anyway, a threat needs no keyword (no evidence)
  • allowed outcomes: refund_partial, no_action, escalate; amount = Σ extra_km of non-exempt segments × $1.4101/km (distance_fare ÷ distance_km), rounded to cents
  • flag_account required: only if a segment is found non-exempt (RD-6 prior upheld dispute on file)

Partial refund $3.00 · account flag → a person

Refund $3.00 as a Ryde voucher for the unexempted off-route segment (2.13 km of metered distance) and send the driver's account to Ryde for review under RD-6.

  • F1 Actual distance 10.7 km vs optimal route 8.4 km: +2.3 km (+27%).

Rider's advocate
  • The ride was a RydeTAXI metered trip (F6 shows base + distance + time units totalling $24.80), so extra kilometres driven flow straight into the fare and any unexcused deviation is refundable per RD-2's metered branch.
  • The GPS record shows the trip covered 10.7 km against an optimal 8.4 km, i.e. +2.3 km (+27%) more than the shortest and most direct route (F1), which the driver was obliged to take absent reasonable cause.
Driver's advocate
  • The trip is RydeTAXI and was charged on the meter ($24.80, distance 10.74 km), so any route-deviation remedy is limited to the metered mileage units of an unexempted segment - not to the whole $3.26 excess shown in the fare breakdown.
  • Only one off-route segment exists (12:12–12:16), adding 2.13 km; the remaining 0.18 km of the +2.31 km excess is map-matching/loop noise that RD-2 expressly leaves unrefunded.

No-show fee · B-0026

No-show · "I'm Here" tapped 600 m away

23.9 s
Dispute statement

The driver marked arrived but was never at the pickup point. I waited there the whole time and then got charged $6.61 as a no-show.

Trip summary

RydeX Bugis Junction → Tanjong Pagar Centre, accepted 12:02:00, cancelled by driver at 12:09:25; cancellation fee $6.61

Proposed outcome

Full refund $6.61 · account flag → a person

Refund the $6.61 cancellation fee to the rider as a Ryde voucher (NS-7) and send the driver's account to Ryde review for the false arrival confirmation; + flag_account

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Driver's closest approach to pickup: 599 m at 12:06:55. Driver never came within 200 m.
  • F2 Driver stationary 12:05:55–12:09:25 (3 min 30 s) at (1.3048, 103.8549), 600 m from pickup.
  • F3 Accepted 12:02:00. Cancelled by driver at 12:09:25 (7 min 25 s after acceptance). Cancellation fee on record: $6.61.
  • F4 driver_arrived_at is null: the platform never recorded the driver at the pickup point.
  • F5 Driver tapped "I'm Here" at 12:06:15, 600 m from the pickup point at that moment; stayed within 200 m for 0 s after the tap.
  • F6 Cancellation fee charged: $6.61.
  • F7 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F8 Chat #1 12:06 driver: “I'm here already, silver Toyota Altis, please come out”
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The driver tapped "I'm Here" at 12:06:15 while 600 m from the pickup point, so requirement (ii) of NS-1 fails: at the moment of the tap the GPS record does not place the driver at the pickup location.
  • NS-1 requirement (iii) also fails: after tapping "I'm Here" the driver spent 0 seconds within 200 m of the pickup point, and the driver's closest approach for the whole trip was 599 m, so there was no continuous 3-minute wait at the pickup before cancelling.
Driver's advocate
  • The cancellation fee of $6.61 stands only if the driver's no-show claim satisfies NS-1, and the record shows his "I'm Here" tap was made 600 m from the pickup point, so the NS-1 (ii) location requirement is not met.
  • The platform never recorded the driver arriving at the pickup point (driver_arrived_at is null) and his closest approach during the whole trip was 599 m, so he was never within 200 m of the pickup point at any time.
Policy trace
  • ✓ NS-0 who cancelled [case] cancelled_by = driver (F3)
  • ✓ NS-1 GPS trace covers the wait without a hole [case] samples run from the arrival confirmation to the cancellation with no gap beyond the hole threshold (F5)
  • ✓ NS-1 (i) arrival confirmed: driver tapped I'm Here, or the platform auto-confirmed arrival [case] driver_tap at 12:06:15 (F5)
  • ✗ NS-1 (ii) within 200 m of pickup AT THE MOMENT of the arrival confirmation [case] 600 m from pickup at the driver_tap (closest approach overall 599 m) (F5, F1)
  • ✗ NS-1 (iii) stayed at the pickup more than 3 min after the arrival confirmation, until cancelling (NS-1 default: more than 3 min) [case] 0 s continuously inside the radius after the driver_tap at 12:06:15; threshold 180 s (>) (F5)
  • ✓ NS-2 arrival confirmed while not at pickup → flag_account [case] fake arrival confirmation (F5, F1)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. no hint word; read the messages anyway, a threat needs no keyword (no evidence)
  • allowed outcomes: refund_full, escalate; amount = cancellation fee $6.61
  • flag_account required: True
Why it was cleared

All checks passed: the policy trace allows this outcome and the amount matches what the code computed. The refund is cleared for voucher issuance. The account flag is not cleared: it goes to a person in the trust and safety queue.

What would change this ruling
  • Had NS-1 (ii) and (iii) both held ((ii) within 200 m of pickup AT THE MOMENT of the arrival confirmation; (iii) stayed at the pickup more than 3 min after the arrival confirmation, until cancelling (NS-1 default: more than 3 min)), the fee would stand and no refund would be due.
Notices
Notice to the rider
We reviewed your cancellation dispute and you are getting the $6.61 cancellation fee back in full, paid as a Ryde voucher. The fee did not stand up: the driver tapped "I'm Here" while about 600 m from the pickup point and never came within 200 m of it, so the arrival that triggers a no-show fee was not a real one. You were at the pickup point and said so in chat. Nothing else is needed from you, and no change is being made to your account.
Notice to the driver
We reviewed the cancellation on your trip and the record shows you tapped "I'm Here" while still about 600 m from the pickup point, and you never came within 200 m of it - your closest point was 599 m. Because of that, the rider's no-show fee is being refunded to them as a $6.61 Ryde voucher. No action is needed from you right now.

Route deviation · B-0060

Detour · two segs consent seg1 pa

25.4 s
Dispute statement

I agreed to go round the jam near the start, but later he went off the route a second time without asking. I want that part refunded.

Trip summary

RydeTAXI Tampines Mall → Orchard Road, estimate 16.3 km / 23.4 min, pickup 17:00 drop-off 17:33; charged $44.69 (metered)

Proposed outcome

Partial refund $3.64 · account flag → a person

Refund $3.64 to the rider as a Ryde voucher (NS-7) for the unexempted F4 detour; note the RD-1 breach on file for the driver and open an account review referral upon execution.

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Actual distance 21.7 km vs optimal route 16.3 km: +5.4 km (+33%).
  • F2 Trip duration 33.8 min vs estimated 23.4 min (+10.4 min).
  • F3 Off-route segment 17:04–17:11: 4.09 km driven where the optimal route covers 1.64 km, adding 2.44 km (up to 785 m from the optimal route).
  • F4 Off-route segment 17:18–17:23: 3.48 km driven where the optimal route covers 0.89 km, adding 2.58 km (up to 1163 m from the optimal route).
  • F5 Excess distance billed $7.56 (5.36 km × $1.4100/km, this trip's distance fare ÷ distance km).
  • F6 Waiting fee billed $0.13 (0.5 min × $0.26/min).
  • F7 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F8 Total charged $44.69 SGD: base $3.90, distance $30.54 (21.66 km), time $8.65 (33.2 min), waiting $0.13, platform $1.47, discount $0.00.
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The trip was a RydeTAXI metered trip in which the driver drove 21.7 km against a 16.3 km optimal route (+5.4 km, +33%, 33.8 min vs 23.4 min estimated), so mileage above the shortest direct route enters the metered fare.
  • The record contains two off-route segments: F3 (17:04–17:11, 4.09 km driven vs 1.64 km optimal, adding 2.44 km) and F4 (17:18–17:23, 3.48 km driven vs 0.89 km optimal, adding 2.58 km); each is an unexempted detour unless a clause excuses it.
Driver's advocate
  • Segment 1 (17:04–17:11, F3) was preceded by the rider's own consent message: at 17:01 the driver asked whether he could go round the jam via Bedok North Road (F9) and at 17:02 the rider replied 'Sure, your call.' (F10), a general route choice made before the segment started.
  • The reroute at the start was not a pure driver preference: the expressway route carried speed band 4 (30–39 km/h) congestion during 16:49–17:38, i.e. through both segments (F13, F14), which is the congestion the driver flagged at 17:01 (F9).
Policy trace
  • ✓ RD-1 off-route segment(s) exist [case] 2 segment(s) (F3, F4)
  • ✗ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F3] no qualifying incident (speed band alone does not exempt) (no evidence)
  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F3] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 17:04:50 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount. Evidence - F9: Chat #1 17:01 driver: “Traffic ahead on Pan Island Expressway is building up, ok if I go round via Bedok North Road?”; F10: Chat #2 17:02 rider: “Sure, your call.”; F12: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “I asked about going round the jam and the rider agreed. The second turn-off was my GPS rerouting.” (F9, F10, F12)
  • ? RD-3 rider-requested stop; whether the rider named this place [F3] RULE (RD-3(a)): exempt if a RIDER message before the segment named a specific place and the car stopped there (the whole way there is exempt; how long the stop lasted or how far the place was does not reduce it). A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (F9, F10)
  • ✗ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F4] no qualifying incident (speed band alone does not exempt) (no evidence)
  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F4] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 17:18:05 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount. The second turn-off was my GPS rerouting.” (F9, F10, F12)
  • ? A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (F9, F10)

  • ?   • allowed outcomes: refund_partial, no_action, escalate; amount = Σ extra_km of non-exempt segments × $1.4100/km (distance_fare ÷ distance_km), rounded to cents

Why it was cleared

All checks passed: the policy trace allows this outcome and the amount matches what the code computed. The refund is cleared for voucher issuance. The account flag is not cleared: it goes to a person in the trust and safety queue.

What would change this ruling
  • F3: the judge found the rider's own message covers this segment, so it is exempt; without that message $3.44 would be refunded.
  • F4: the judge found no rider agreement covering this segment, so $3.64 is refunded; a rider message agreeing to it before it started would have exempted it.
Notices
Notice to the rider
Good news - your fare dispute is settled.

We looked at the trip and found two stretches where the driver went off route. One was fine: the driver asked if he could go round via Bedok North Road and you said "Sure, your call."

The second one wasn't. That turn-off happened about 15 minutes later, and there's no message from you agreeing to it. The driver himself said it was his own GPS rerouting, and there was no traffic hold-up or bad weather to explain it.

So you're getting back $3.64 as a Ryde voucher for the extra distance on that stretch. Your waiting charge and the rest of the fare stay as billed.
Notice to the driver
This dispute has been decided against you.

Only one of the two off-route stretches counted against you. The first was covered - you asked the rider about going round via Bedok North Road and they said "Sure, your call."

The second stretch, about 15 minutes later, wasn't covered by any agreement from the rider, and your own post-trip explanation was that it came from your GPS rerouting. No traffic incident or bad weather supported it.

$3.64 goes to the rider as a Ryde voucher, and the off-route breach stays on your file. Because this is a repeated route issue on your account, we're opening an account review.

Route deviation · B-0001

Detour · unexplained loop

22.6 s
Dispute statement

The app estimated 21 km; the driver turned off the route without a word and the meter read 24 km at the end. Please refund the extra distance.

Trip summary

RydeTAXI Raffles Place → Changi Airport T3, estimate 21.2 km / 28.4 min, pickup 09:07 drop-off 09:43; charged $49.37 (metered)

Proposed outcome

Partial refund $4.33

Issue a $4.33 Ryde voucher to the rider (RydeTAXI metered-distance overcharge for the unexempted 09:24–09:30 segment); no account action against the driver.

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Actual distance 24.4 km vs optimal route 21.2 km: +3.2 km (+15%).
  • F2 Trip duration 36.8 min vs estimated 28.4 min (+8.4 min).
  • F3 Off-route segment 09:24–09:30: 3.78 km driven where the optimal route covers 0.71 km, adding 3.07 km (up to 688 m from the optimal route).
  • F4 Excess distance billed $4.51 (3.20 km × $1.4101/km, this trip's distance fare ÷ distance km).
  • F5 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F6 Total charged $49.37 SGD: base $3.90, distance $34.42 (24.41 km), time $9.58 (36.8 min), waiting $0.00, platform $1.47, discount $0.00.
  • F7 Chat #1 09:04 rider: “Hi, I am at the Raffles Place taxi stand, with a black backpack.”
  • F8 Chat #2 09:05 driver: “2 min away, blue Toyota Prius.”
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The GPS record shows the trip ran 24.4 km against an optimal 21.2 km, i.e. +3.2 km (+15%), and the only off-route segment is 09:24–09:30, where the car drove 3.78 km where the optimal route covers 0.71 km, adding 3.07 km and straying up to 688 m from the optimal route - an unexplained deviation.
  • Nothing in the chat record asks for or agrees to the deviation: the two timestamped messages are only the rider's pick-up description at 09:04 and the driver's '2 min away' at 09:05, both before the segment, neither naming a road or a stop, so no rider consent under RD-5 covers the 09:24–09:30 segment.
Driver's advocate
  • The route facts show one, and only one, off-route segment (F3): 09:24–09:30, 3.78 km driven where the optimal route covers 0.71 km, adding 3.07 km; no other segment appears in the record, so the only mileage in dispute is that single segment.
  • The chat record contains only two timestamped messages (F7 rider at the taxi stand, F8 driver '2 min away'), and neither the rider nor the driver ever named a road or a stop before the segment, so no rider consent exists for the segment and my client does not claim any.
Policy trace
  • ✓ RD-1 off-route segment(s) exist [case] 1 segment(s) (F3)
  • ✗ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F3] no qualifying incident (speed band alone does not exempt) (no evidence)
  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F3] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 09:24:50 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount. Evidence - F7: Chat #1 09:04 rider: “Hi, I am at the Raffles Place taxi stand, with a black backpack.”; F8: Chat #2 09:05 driver: “2 min away, blue Toyota Prius.”; F10: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “I went where the GPS sent me; it rerouted. The fare is what the meter showed.” (F7, F8, F10)
  • ? RD-3 rider-requested stop; whether the rider named this place [F3] RULE (RD-3(a)): exempt if a RIDER message before the segment named a specific place and the car stopped there (the whole way there is exempt; how long the stop lasted or how far the place was does not reduce it). A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (F7, F8)
  • ✗ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 0 (F16)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. no hint word; read the messages anyway, a threat needs no keyword (no evidence)
  • allowed outcomes: refund_partial, no_action, escalate; amount = Σ extra_km of non-exempt segments × $1.4101/km (distance_fare ÷ distance_km), rounded to cents
  • flag_account required: False
Why it was cleared

All checks passed: the policy trace allows this outcome and the amount matches what the code computed. The refund is cleared for voucher issuance.

What would change this ruling
  • F3: the judge found no rider agreement covering this segment, so $4.33 is refunded; a rider message agreeing to it before it started would have exempted it.
  • Had the driver carried a prior upheld route dispute in the last 90 days, RD-6 would add an account flag.
Notices
Notice to the rider
We reviewed your dispute and found in your favour. The drive from 09:24 to 09:30 added about 3.07 km over the shortest route, and nothing on file excuses it - there was no traffic incident nearby, the recorded speeds were normal for the roads, and no message from you agreed to a different route. Because your trip was metered by distance, that extra distance raised your fare. We're issuing you a $4.33 Ryde voucher, which will appear in your Ryde account. No action is being taken against the driver's account. Other distance billed on the trip isn't part of this refund.
Notice to the driver
We reviewed a rider dispute about the route taken between 09:24 and 09:30. The records show about 3.07 km driven beyond the shortest route in that stretch, and nothing on file excuses it: no traffic incident nearby, normal recorded speeds for those roads, and no rider message agreeing to a different route. As the trip was metered by distance, that extra distance was refunded to the rider as a $4.33 Ryde voucher. No action is being taken on your account. Please follow the shortest practical route unless a rider agrees otherwise in chat.

  • F1 Actual distance 10.6 km vs optimal route 8.4 km: +2.2 km (+26%).

  • F4 Excess distance billed $3.06 (2.17 km × $1.4104/km, this trip's distance fare ÷ distance km).
  • F5 Waiting fee billed $0.11 (0.4 min × $0.26/min).

What each side argued
Rider's advocate

  • There is an off-route segment 14:28–14:34 that drove 3.07 km where the optimal route covers 1.12 km, adding 1.94 km and straying up to 786 m from the optimal route (F3); it is not exempted by RD-3 because the rider named no stop and no detour route, and RD-4 cannot exempt it because F15 records no traffic incident near the route.
Driver's advocate

(F8) and the rider replied 'Sure, no problem.' (F9), so under RD-5 the segment is not a deviation.
  • The rider's consent message F9 is timestamped 14:31, which is after the F3 segment start of 14:28, so on its face the message does not satisfy RD-5's 'before the segment' requirement and that timing point must be conceded rather than argued.

Route deviation · B-0004

Detour · incident on the replaced stretch

35.6 s
Dispute statement

The driver left the route the app showed and the meter was higher than the estimate. I never agreed to any change.

Trip summary

RydeTAXI Woodlands Civic Centre → Punggol Waterway Point, estimate 17.2 km / 23.6 min, pickup 17:28 drop-off 18:01; charged $45.52 (metered)

Proposed outcome

No refund

Close the fare-review request: the sole off-route segment (17:38–17:49) is exempt under RD-4(a) on the LTA incident record, the metered excess (5.10 km / $7.19, F4) is therefore not refundable, and no account action applies.

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Actual distance 22.2 km vs optimal route 17.2 km: +5.0 km (+30%).
  • F2 Trip duration 33.8 min vs estimated 23.6 min (+10.2 min).
  • F3 Off-route segment 17:38–17:49: 6.56 km driven where the optimal route covers 1.64 km, adding 4.92 km (up to 1911 m from the optimal route).
  • F4 Excess distance billed $7.19 (5.10 km × $1.4099/km, this trip's distance fare ÷ distance km).
  • F5 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F6 Total charged $45.52 SGD: base $3.90, distance $31.37 (22.25 km), time $8.78 (33.8 min), waiting $0.00, platform $1.47, discount $0.00.
  • F7 Chat #1 17:23 rider: “I'm at the main entrance already, in a grey hoodie.”
  • F8 Chat #2 17:24 driver: “On my way, about 4 min, white Honda Vezel.”
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The GPS track shows one off-route segment 17:38–17:49 in which 6.56 km was driven where the optimal route covers 1.64 km, adding 4.92 km and drifting up to 1911 m from the optimal route; that segment is an unexempted deviation under RD-1.
  • The whole trip ran 22.2 km against a 17.2 km optimal route (+5.0 km, +30%) and 33.8 min against 23.6 min estimated (F1, F2); the driver's metered fare rose accordingly, to $45.52 with $31.37 of distance charge on 22.25 km (F6).
Driver's advocate
  • The trip is metered RydeTAXI (F6: base $3.90 + distance $31.37 + time $8.78 + platform $1.47 = $45.52), so any refund can only be the metered mileage attributable to a non-exempt off-route segment, computed at this trip's per-km rate of $1.4099/km (F4, F6); the rider's complaint about 'the meter being higher than the estimate' cannot support a refund of the quoted estimate, because RydeTAXI is expressly metered and not a fixed quote.
  • There is exactly one off-route segment in the record: 17:38–17:49, 6.56 km driven where the optimal route covers 1.64 km, adding 4.92 km (F3); the excess distance billed was 5.10 km = $7.19 at $1.4099/km (F4), so the whole disputed extra mileage is that single segment.
Policy trace
  • ✓ RD-1 off-route segment(s) exist [case] 1 segment(s) (F3)
  • ✓ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F3] incident within 300 m of optimal km 7.1–8.7 (F13)
  • – RD-5 consent scope [F3] not needed: RD-4 already exempts this segment (no evidence)
  • – RD-3 rider-requested stop [F3] not needed: RD-4 already exempts this segment (no evidence)
  • ✗ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 0 (F16)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. no hint word; read the messages anyway, a threat needs no keyword (no evidence)
  • allowed outcomes: no_action, escalate; amount = Σ extra_km of non-exempt segments × $1.4099/km (distance_fare ÷ distance_km), rounded to cents
  • flag_account required: False
Why it was cleared

All checks passed: the policy trace allows this outcome and the amount matches what the code computed. No refund is due.

  • Had the driver carried a prior upheld route dispute in the last 90 days, RD-6 would add an account flag.

  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F3] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 18:46:55 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. Rider's advocate

  • The GPS track shows a single off-route segment from 09:13 to 09:24 covering 6.58 km where the optimal route covers 1.57 km - an excess of 5.01 km, and up to 1578 m from the optimal route - which under RD-1 is an unauthorised deviation because no exemption applies.
Driver's advocate
  • The trip is RydeTAXI and metered, so the extra 5.01 km on the off-route segment (F3) is the only measure of any overcharge: the fare facts show excess distance billed of $7.53 (5.34 km × $1.4098/km, F4) on a total of $34.83 (F6); under RD-2 the most that could ever be refunded is that distance component, nothing else.
  • The single off-route segment ran 09:13–09:24 and covered 6.58 km where the optimal route covers 1.57 km (F3), i.e. the deviation occurred inside the window when the Ayer Rajah Expressway was logged at speed band 2, 10–19 km/h, from 08:49 to 09:13 (F9) - corroborating congestion on the replaced optimal route rather than a driver errand.
Policy trace
  • ✓ RD-1 off-route segment(s) exist [case] 1 segment(s) (F3)
  • ✗ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F3] no qualifying incident (speed band alone does not exempt) (no evidence)
  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F3] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 09:13:55 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount. Evidence - F8: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “Ayer Rajah Expressway was crawling and flooding in the heavy rain, so I went round it. Any driver would have done the same.” (F8)
  • ? RD-3 rider-requested stop; whether the rider named this place [F3] RULE (RD-3(a)): exempt if a RIDER message before the segment named a specific place and the car stopped there (the whole way there is exempt; how long the stop lasted or how far the place was does not reduce it). A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (no evidence)
  • ✗ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 0 (F15)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. no hint word; read the messages anyway, a threat needs no keyword (no evidence)
  • allowed outcomes: refund_partial, no_action, escalate; amount = Σ extra_km of non-exempt segments × $1.4098/km (distance_fare ÷ distance_km), rounded to cents
  • flag_account required: False
Why it was cleared

All checks passed: the policy trace allows this outcome and the amount matches what the code computed. The refund is cleared for voucher issuance.

What would change this ruling
  • F3: the judge found no rider agreement covering this segment, so $7.06 is refunded; a rider message agreeing to it before it started would have exempted it.
  • Had the driver carried a prior upheld route dispute in the last 90 days, RD-6 would add an account flag.

Nothing in the traffic or weather records excuses that stretch. The voucher covers that distance only and will be added to your Ryde account. Your account is not affected.

  • ✗ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F4] no qualifying incident (speed band alone does not exempt) (F12)
  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F4] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 22:02:05 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount. Evidence - F9: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “There was an accident on Kallang Paya Lebar Expressway ahead, lanes were blocked, so I went round it. The extra distance was unavoidable.” (F9)
  • ? RD-3 rider-requested stop; whether the rider named this place [F4] RULE (RD-3(a)): exempt if a RIDER message before the segment named a specific place and the car stopped there (the whole way there is exempt; how long the stop lasted or how far the place was does not reduce it). A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (no evidence)
  • ✗ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 0 (F15)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. no hint word; read the messages anyway, a threat needs no keyword (no evidence)
  • allowed outcomes: refund_partial, no_action, escalate; amount = Σ extra_km of non-exempt segments × $1.4101/km (distance_fare ÷ distance_km), rounded to cents
  • flag_account required: False

(2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes no amount (fixed fare) but decides the RD-6 flag (the driver has a prior). Evidence - F8: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “The GPS rerouted me, that's all. Nothing wrong with the fare.” (F8)
  • ? RD-3 rider-requested stop; whether the rider named this place [F3] RULE (RD-3(a)): exempt if a RIDER message before the segment named a specific place and the car stopped there (the whole way there is exempt; how long the stop lasted or how far the place was does not reduce it). A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (no evidence)
  • ✗ RD-2 metered service (detour km enters the fare) [case] RydeX is a fixed fare (no evidence)
  • ✓ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 2 (F14)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. no hint word; read the messages anyway, a threat needs no keyword (no evidence)
  • allowed outcomes: no_action, escalate
  • flag_account required: only if a segment is found non-exempt (RD-6 prior upheld dispute on file)
Why it was cleared

All checks passed: the policy trace allows this outcome and the amount matches what the code computed. No refund is due. The account flag is not cleared: it goes to a person in the trust and safety queue.

What would change this ruling
  • F3: the judge found no rider agreement covering this segment, so the RD-1 breach is recorded; a rider message agreeing to it before it started would have exempted it.

No-show fee · B-0026

23.9 s
Dispute statement

The driver marked arrived but was never at the pickup point. I waited there the whole time and then got charged $6.61 as a no-show.

Trip summary

RydeX Bugis Junction → Tanjong Pagar Centre, accepted 12:02:00, cancelled by driver at 12:09:25; cancellation fee $6.61

Proposed outcome

Full refund $6.61 · account flag → a person

Refund the $6.61 cancellation fee to the rider as a Ryde voucher (NS-7) and send the driver's account to Ryde review for the false arrival confirmation; + flag_account

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Driver's closest approach to pickup: 599 m at 12:06:55. Driver never came within 200 m.
  • F2 Driver stationary 12:05:55–12:09:25 (3 min 30 s) at (1.3048, 103.8549), 600 m from pickup.
  • F3 Accepted 12:02:00. Cancelled by driver at 12:09:25 (7 min 25 s after acceptance). Cancellation fee on record: $6.61.
  • F4 driver_arrived_at is null: the platform never recorded the driver at the pickup point.
  • F5 Driver tapped "I'm Here" at 12:06:15, 600 m from the pickup point at that moment; stayed within 200 m for 0 s after the tap.
  • F6 Cancellation fee charged: $6.61.
  • F7 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F8 Chat #1 12:06 driver: “I'm here already, silver Toyota Altis, please come out”
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The driver tapped "I'm Here" at 12:06:15 while 600 m from the pickup point, so requirement (ii) of NS-1 fails: at the moment of the tap the GPS record does not place the driver at the pickup location.
  • NS-1 requirement (iii) also fails: after tapping "I'm Here" the driver spent 0 seconds within 200 m of the pickup point, and the driver's closest approach for the whole trip was 599 m, so there was no continuous 3-minute wait at the pickup before cancelling.
Driver's advocate
  • The cancellation fee of $6.61 stands only if the driver's no-show claim satisfies NS-1, and the record shows his "I'm Here" tap was made 600 m from the pickup point, so the NS-1 (ii) location requirement is not met.
  • The platform never recorded the driver arriving at the pickup point (driver_arrived_at is null) and his closest approach during the whole trip was 599 m, so he was never within 200 m of the pickup point at any time.
Policy trace
  • ✓ NS-0 who cancelled [case] cancelled_by = driver (F3)
  • ✓ NS-1 GPS trace covers the wait without a hole [case] samples run from the arrival confirmation to the cancellation with no gap beyond the hole threshold (F5)

  • ✗ NS-1 (ii) within 200 m of pickup AT THE MOMENT of the arrival confirmation [case] 600 m from pickup at the driver_tap (closest approach overall 599 m) (F5, F1)
  • ✗ NS-1 (iii) stayed at the pickup more than 3 min after the arrival confirmation, until cancelling (NS-1 default: more than 3 min) [case] 0 s continuously inside the radius after the driver_tap at 12:06:15; threshold 180 s (>) (F5)
  • ✓ NS-2 arrival confirmed while not at pickup → flag_account [case] fake arrival confirmation (F5, F1)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. no hint word; read the messages anyway, a threat needs no keyword (no evidence)
  • allowed outcomes: refund_full, escalate; amount = cancellation fee $6.61
  • flag_account required: True
Why it was cleared

All checks passed: the policy trace allows this outcome and the amount matches what the code computed. The refund is cleared for voucher issuance. The account flag is not cleared: it goes to a person in the trust and safety queue.

What would change this ruling
  • Had NS-1 (ii) and (iii) both held ((ii) within 200 m of pickup AT THE MOMENT of the arrival confirmation; (iii) stayed at the pickup more than 3 min after the arrival confirmation, until cancelling (NS-1 default: more than 3 min)), the fee would stand and no refund would be due.

Because of that, the rider's no-show fee is being refunded to them as a $6.61 Ryde voucher. No-show fee · B-0027

No-show · driver waited only 2 min

27.9 s
Dispute statement

The driver cancelled on me after barely two minutes and I was charged $6.61.

Trip summary

RydeX Bedok Mall → Raffles Place, accepted 14:09:00, cancelled by driver at 14:15:50; cancellation fee $6.61

Proposed outcome

Full refund $6.61

Refund the $6.61 cancellation fee to the rider as a Ryde voucher under NS-7; no account flag (NS-2's flag only applies to a fake arrival confirmation, which the trace marks ✗).

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Driver's closest approach to pickup: 38 m at 14:15:35.
  • F2 Driver first within 200 m of pickup at 14:13:25; cumulative time within 200 m before cancellation: 2 min 25 s.
  • F3 Driver stationary 14:13:50–14:15:50 (2 min 0 s) at (1.3252, 103.9299), 41 m from pickup.
  • F4 Accepted 14:09:00. Cancelled by driver at 14:15:50 (6 min 50 s after acceptance). Cancellation fee on record: $6.61.
  • F5 Platform recorded driver arrival at 14:13:25; cancelled 2 min 25 s after arrival.
  • F6 Driver tapped "I'm Here" at 14:14:10, 41 m from the pickup point at that moment; stayed within 200 m for 1 min 40 s after the tap.
  • F7 Cancellation fee charged: $6.61.
  • F8 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The driver cancelled the ride as a rider no-show, so the $6.61 cancellation fee stands or falls on NS-1's three conditions (arrival tap, being at the pickup at that moment, and more than 3 continuous minutes of waiting afterwards).
  • Condition (iii) of NS-1 fails: after tapping "I'm Here" at 14:14:10 the driver remained within 200 m of the pickup for only 1 min 40 s (100 s) before cancelling at 14:15:50, well short of the required 3 min 01 s (the default >3-minute threshold).

(2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. This finding changes the amount. Rider's advocate

  • The rider's consent was solicited before the change, not after: the driver asked at 15:17 whether to take another way because traffic on PIE was building up (F8), the rider answered at 15:18 (F9), and the segment starts at 15:21 (F3) - the sequence satisfies RD-5's 'before you go' requirement, and the chat record is complete with every message timestamped (F10).
Policy trace
  • ✓ RD-1 off-route segment(s) exist [case] 1 segment(s) (F3)
  • ✗ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F3] no qualifying incident (speed band alone does not exempt) (no evidence)
  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F3] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 15:21:15 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount. A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (F8, F9)

Evidence - F9: Chat #1 17:01 driver: “Traffic ahead on Pan Island Expressway is building up, ok if I go round via Bedok North Road?”; F10: Chat #2 17:02 rider: “Sure, your call.”; F12: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “I asked about going round the jam and the rider agreed. The second turn-off was my GPS rerouting.” (F9, F10, F12)
  • ? RD-3 rider-requested stop; whether the rider named this place [F4] RULE (RD-3(a)): exempt if a RIDER message before the segment named a specific place and the car stopped there (the whole way there is exempt; how long the stop lasted or how far the place was does not reduce it). A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (F9, F10)
  • ✓ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 2 (F18)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. no hint word; read the messages anyway, a threat needs no keyword (no evidence)
  • allowed outcomes: refund_partial, no_action, escalate; amount = Σ extra_km of non-exempt segments × $1.4100/km (distance_fare ÷ distance_km), rounded to cents
  • flag_account required: only if a segment is found non-exempt (RD-6 prior upheld dispute on file)
Why it was cleared

All checks passed: the policy trace allows this outcome and the amount matches what the code computed. The refund is cleared for voucher issuance. The account flag is not cleared: it goes to a person in the trust and safety queue.

What would change this ruling
  • F3: the judge found the rider's own message covers this segment, so it is exempt; without that message $3.44 would be refunded.
  • F4: the judge found no rider agreement covering this segment, so $3.64 is refunded; a rider message agreeing to it before it started would have exempted it.
Notices
Notice to the rider
Good news - your fare dispute is settled.

We looked at the trip and found two stretches where the driver went off route. One was fine: the driver asked if he could go round via Bedok North Road and you said "Sure, your call."

The second one wasn't. That turn-off happened about 15 minutes later, and there's no message from you agreeing to it. The driver himself said it was his own GPS rerouting, and there was no traffic hold-up or bad weather to explain it.

So you're getting back $3.64 as a Ryde voucher for the extra distance on that stretch. Your waiting charge and the rest of the fare stay as billed.
Notice to the driver
This dispute has been decided against you.

Only one of the two off-route stretches counted against you. The first was covered - you asked the rider about going round via Bedok North Road and they said "Sure, your call."

The second stretch, about 15 minutes later, wasn't covered by any agreement from the rider, and your own post-trip explanation was that it came from your GPS rerouting. No traffic incident or bad weather supported it.

$3.64 goes to the rider as a Ryde voucher, and the off-route breach stays on your file. Because this is a repeated route issue on your account, we're opening an account review.

Route deviation · B-0062

Detour · fixed fare consent cr

25.8 s
Dispute statement

The driver took a longer way round than the app showed. The fare was a fixed quote, but I want the detour looked at.

Trip summary

RydeX Compass One → Raffles Place, estimate 17.6 km / 25.8 min, pickup 21:14 drop-off 21:47; charged $36.95 (fixed)

Proposed outcome

No refund

Close the case as no action on the fare (fixed RydeX quote, billed distance equals the optimal route), and note in the trip record that one off-route segment (F3) was driven under a rider-agreed named-road reroute whose correspondence to Ang Mo Kio Avenue 5 is not established by any fact (RD-5, G13), while confirming no refundable add-on appears in the fare breakdown.

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Actual distance 20.5 km vs optimal route 17.6 km: +2.9 km (+16%).
  • F2 Trip duration 33.8 min vs estimated 25.8 min (+8.0 min).
  • F3 Off-route segment 21:25–21:32: 4.46 km driven where the optimal route covers 2.34 km, adding 2.12 km (up to 1256 m from the optimal route).
  • F4 Billed distance 17.6 km does not exceed the optimal route (17.6 km).
  • F5 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F6 Total charged $36.95 SGD: base $3.90, distance $24.87 (17.64 km), time $6.71 (25.8 min), waiting $0.00, platform $1.47, discount $0.00.
  • F7 Chat #1 21:21 driver: “Traffic ahead on Central Expressway is building up, ok if I go round via Ang Mo Kio Avenue 5?”
  • F8 Chat #2 21:22 rider: “Sure, no problem.”
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The GPS track shows one off-route segment, 21:25–21:32, in which the car drove 4.46 km where the optimal route covers 2.34 km, adding 2.12 km and straying up to 1256 m from the optimal route; that segment is a route deviation under RD-1.
  • The whole trip ran 20.5 km against an optimal 17.6 km (+2.9 km, +16%) and 33.8 min against an estimated 25.8 min, confirming the complaint that the driver took a longer way round (F1, F2).
Driver's advocate
  • There is exactly one off-route segment in this trip: segment 21:25–21:32, 4.46 km driven where the optimal route covers 2.34 km, adding 2.12 km (F3).
  • Before that segment began at 21:25, the driver proposed a specific alternative in chat: 'Traffic ahead on Central Expressway is building up, ok if I go round via Ang Mo Kio Avenue 5?' sent at 21:21, and the rider replied 'Sure, no problem.' at 21:22 (F7, F8). The rider's own message predates the segment start, so RD-5 consent exists in time.
Policy trace
  • ✓ RD-1 off-route segment(s) exist [case] 1 segment(s) (F3)
  • ✗ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F3] no qualifying incident (speed band alone does not exempt) (no evidence)
  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F3] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 21:25:10 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes no amount (fixed fare) and no flag: if you cannot settle it, undetermined is allowed and the case may still be ruled. Evidence - F7: Chat #1 21:21 driver: “Traffic ahead on Central Expressway is building up, ok if I go round via Ang Mo Kio Avenue 5?”; F8: Chat #2 21:22 rider: “Sure, no problem.”; F10: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “Traffic was building up ahead so I asked the rider before turning off via Ang Mo Kio Avenue 5 and the rider agreed.” (F7, F8, F10)
  • ? RD-3 rider-requested stop; whether the rider named this place [F3] RULE (RD-3(a)): exempt if a RIDER message before the segment named a specific place and the car stopped there (the whole way there is exempt; how long the stop lasted or how far the place was does not reduce it). A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (F7, F8)
  • ✗ RD-2 metered service (detour km enters the fare) [case] RydeX is a fixed fare (no evidence)
  • ✗ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 0 (F17)
